.claude\rules\codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:26:## The rule (two empirical Forward Disciplines for codex T1 Path P prompt construction)
.claude\rules\codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:28:Codex T1 Path P prompts must be SCOPE-CONTROLLED to prevent Pattern B HONEST-NON-FINDING (HNF) verdicts from budget exhaustion. Two empirical sub-rules cover two distinct fire classes.
.claude\rules\codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:33:- `repo_size_kb > 200,000` (200MB) OR
.claude\rules\codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:36:THEN build TIGHTER Path P prompt scoped to ONE primitive rather than full repo coverage.
.claude\rules\codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:65:| Path P research audit on small repo (<50MB, ≤5 independently-reviewable sub-packages, single-platform) | NEITHER | Default 300s research-fire budget suffices |
.claude\rules\codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:66:| Path P research audit on large monorepo or multi-platform | **#1 only** | Scope to single primitive |
.claude\rules\codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:67:| Path P research audit on TIER-1-OFFICIAL with extensive ecosystem | **#1 only** | Scope-tighten despite TIER-1 provenance |
.claude\rules\codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:80:For every codex T1 Path P prompt, verify:
.claude\rules\codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:82:- [ ] **Target sizing**: estimate target size (LOC / KB / independently-reviewable sub-packages)
.claude\rules\codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:83:- [ ] **Forward Discipline #1 trigger check**: `repo_size_kb > 200,000` OR `sub_package_count > 5` (independently reviewable surfaces)?
.claude\rules\codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:102:- **First-pass research on UNKNOWN repo** where you don't yet know repo size or sub-package count — do an initial GitHub MCP repo metadata probe FIRST, then apply Forward Discipline #1 if thresholds met

.claude\rules\codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:121:- `Z:/claude-sota-installed/CLAUDE.md` cardinal-rule-3 (cross-model consensus workflow) — references this rule for Path P prompt-scope-control discipline
.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:12:description: When iterative codex review (or similar adversarial reviewer) keeps surfacing finer-grained concerns on the same logical surface, decide disposition by confidence trend AND severity gate. Monotone-decline + low/medium severity → ACCEPT-WITH-DOC. Escalation OR unresolved high/critical → REVERT-AND-REMOVE. Owner-accepted high/critical → MANUAL-OVERRIDE (distinct, tracked). Stop the arc at round 5 regardless.
.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:42:Track codex confidence values across the arc. Disposition is determined by BOTH the trend AND a severity gate (iteration ceiling alone cannot bless unresolved material findings).
.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:46:ACCEPT-WITH-DOC is a **hard deny** for any unresolved `high` or `critical` codex severity. No combination of confidence trend, iteration count, or owner signoff can convert an unresolved high/critical finding into ACCEPT-WITH-DOC. (If you want to ship anyway, see the separate MANUAL-OVERRIDE disposition below.)
.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:53:If neither holds (even at medium severity) → REVERT-AND-REMOVE or kick back to fix-iteration regardless of confidence trend. The severity gate is non-negotiable — iteration count and confidence trend alone cannot bless unresolved material findings.
.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:70:### Outcome B — REVERT-AND-REMOVE (escalation OR unresolved high-severity)
.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:82:### Outcome C — MANUAL-OVERRIDE (owner accepts unresolved high/critical risk)
.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:112:3. **ACCEPT-WITH-DOC** ONLY if: last-round severity is `medium` or lower AND severity gate passes (concrete verification OR severity downgrade in evidence) AND confidence declined monotonically. Hard deny for any unresolved `high`/`critical`.
.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:113:4. **MANUAL-OVERRIDE** if: user/owner explicitly accepts unresolved high/critical risk for this specific commit. Distinct from ACCEPT — uses `MANUAL-OVERRIDE` marker, tracked as code-review-burn metric.
.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:114:5. **Escalate to user** if: round ≥ 5 with high-severity unresolved finding, no clear REVERT path (e.g., feature has callers we can't easily migrate), and no owner available for MANUAL-OVERRIDE.

.claude\rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:19:# Reference: TIER-1 ALT-IMPL CLI-runtime reinforcement `Z:/repos/deps/codex/codex-rs/git-utils/src/info.rs:618-654 @ HEAD 993e3f407ea8213f7d32cb9367ae7616b7e15b4a [VERIFIED 2026-05-04 via Wave 18 sota-researcher + codex T1 conf=0.86]` (OpenAI codex `resolve_root_git_project_for_trust` worktree-aware runtime: parses `.git`-file `gitdir:` pointer + walks worktrees-dir → common-dir hierarchy to resolve trust to main repo. Cross-vendor AI-CLI implements same worktree-aware behavior pattern as Claude — strongest practitioner-endorsement evidence shape. .claude\rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:115:   - **(c) Lockfile + O_CREAT|O_APPEND atomic-write for SHARED JSONL append**: open the JSONL log with `os.open(path, os.O_CREAT | os.O_APPEND | os.O_WRONLY, mode)` where `mode` defaults to `0o600` (owner-only — per Round-5b T3 round-3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.78 NEW-CLASS trust-boundary finding on commit `c2b5dee`: `0o644` was world-readable and leaked audit-trail content (agent prompts, PIDs, verdict cite paths, `agent_id` UUIDs, T1/T2/T3 verdict bodies) on multi-user hosts. Callers MAY opt-in to `0o640` group-readable for shared-CI cases via explicit `mode=` argument + documented umask interaction (e.g., `umask 0o027` for CI runner) — `0o644` is REJECTED as default for any audit-trail JSONL). **Migration semantic for legacy files** *(Round-7 amendment per T3 round-4 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.91 NEW-CLASS finding on commit `df37fb1`; closes non-migrating gap caught by codex GPT-5.5 BRIDGE-MODE — POSIX `os.open` applies `mode` ONLY when `O_CREAT` actually CREATES the file)*: pre-existing files at `0o644` (created under Round-3-through-5 mandates pre-Round-6) RETAIN their world-readable perms after open, leaving the audit-trail leak intact unless coerced. After every `os.open`, the caller MUST: (i) read `current_mode = stat.S_IMODE(os.fstat(fd).st_mode)`; (ii) if `current_mode != mode` AND the file is owned by caller-uid, call `os.fchmod(fd, mode)` to coerce existing file to the mode invariant; (iii) if `os.fchmod` raises (`PermissionError` due to read-only mount, alien owner, ACL conflict on Windows NTFS, etc.), FAIL CLOSED — propagate the exception rather than proceeding with non-conforming perms (the audit-trail invariant is enforced everywhere or fails loudly). Document in commit body for any consumer applying this rule: one-time migration shell command `find .claude/state -name '*.jsonl' -perm /044 -exec chmod 0600 {} +` (POSIX) OR `Get-ChildItem -Path .claude/state -Filter *.jsonl -Recurse | Where-Object { ($_ | Get-Acl).AccessToString -match 'Everyone Allow Read' } | ForEach-Object { icacls $_.FullName /inheritance:r /grant ${env:USERNAME}:F }` (Windows) for legacy `mcp_health.jsonl` / `subagent_transcripts.jsonl` / `codex_postcommit_reviews.jsonl` / `subagent_start.jsonl` / `worktree_lifecycle_audit.jsonl` files created at `0o644` per pre-Round-6 mandates. The `O_CREAT` flag ensures a missing log auto-creates rather than failing the first writer; **if `os.open` raises (read-only mount, permission denied, disk full), the exception MUST propagate — do not catch-and-ignore** (per Round-5 F-1 silent-failure finding). Take an **exclusive advisory lock** (`fcntl.flock(fd, LOCK_EX)` on POSIX / `msvcrt.locking` on Windows); **if neither primitive is available, or if the platform does not enforce advisory locks (e.g., NFS without NLM support), FAIL CLOSED — raise rather than proceeding without a lock** (per Round-5 F-2 NFS-silent-succeed finding). **Windows separate-lockfile mechanism** *(Round-7c per T3 round-7b NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.78 NEW-CLASS finding on commit `48c49fb` caught by codex GPT-5.5 BRIDGE-MODE)*: `msvcrt.locking` locks `nbytes` from CURRENT file-pointer position, NOT the whole file like POSIX `fcntl.flock`. Under O_APPEND the JSONL fd's pointer moves on each write, breaking lock-release symmetry (LK_UNLCK at the new pointer would find no lock at the byte LK_LOCK was issued for). Round-7b workaround (shipped at `scripts/_atomic_jsonl_append.py:_acquire_lock(fd, path)` commit `48c49fb`): open separate `<jsonl-path>.lock` file with 1-byte placeholder + lock byte 0 of the LOCKFILE (no writes to lockfile means file pointer stays at byte 0). Module returns `lock_fd` for Windows callers to release; `None` for POSIX. **Lock-protocol rollout constraint** *(Round-7c per same T3 finding)*: when the lock protocol bumps (direct-jsonl-fd → separate-lockfile-v1, or separate-lockfile-v1 → separate-lockfile-v2-versioned), enforce single-version deployment for the entire concurrent-writer cohort. Old + new code locking DIFFERENT lock domains do NOT mutually exclude — concurrent writers can race even though each ALONE acquires its lock correctly. Mitigations (pick ONE before any concurrent rollout): **(i) transitional dual-lock**: acquire both old AND new protocol locks in series during the transition window; **(ii) runtime version-marker**: write protocol-version byte to lockfile + fail-closed on read-version-mismatch (FORWARD-REF for v2 lock protocol bump); **(iii) single-version cohort barrier**: coordinate cohort upgrade via deployment system before any new-version runs. Currently sss has ZERO consumers of `_atomic_jsonl_append.py` (FORWARD-REF wire-up Ships 2-5 pending per `tmp/wave22-orchestrator-salvage-jsonl-mode-bits-audit-2026-05-05.md`), so the version-skew window is THEORETICAL until first hook migrates to use the module. Future protocol bumps MUST honor one of (i)/(ii)/(iii). HOLD the lock across BOTH (a) JSON-encoding the record AND (b) the single low-level `os.write(fd, encoded_bytes)` — never use buffered text writes (`f.write(json.dumps(rec) + "\n")` via Python file objects can split into multiple writes). **If JSON encoding raises (`UnicodeEncodeError`, non-serializable type), release the lock and propagate the exception — do not silently drop the record** (per Round-5 F-3 silent-record-drop finding). For records where **`len(encoded_bytes)` — the byte count AFTER JSON encoding, NOT the key count of the dict —** exceeds the platform atomic-write bound (≤PIPE_BUF 4KB POSIX; ≤512 bytes for Windows or treat the lock as the sole correctness boundary), reject the record OR shard via option (b) filename-namespace; do NOT silently truncate (per Round-5 F-5 measurement-target finding). After the write, `os.fsync(fd)` if durability is part of the contract (audit-trail JSONL needs fsync; ephemeral telemetry may skip); **if `os.fsync` raises (`EIO`, `ENOSPC`), propagate the exception — do not silently ignore a durability failure when durability is contracted** (per Round-5 F-4 fsync-failure finding). Use for SHARED JSONL log targets (`mcp_health.jsonl`, `subagent_transcripts.jsonl`, `codex_postcommit_reviews.jsonl` class) where consumers expect interleaved appends from multiple writers and unified-view semantics are required.

The chosen guard: `_atomic_jsonl_append.py` shared module that wraps every JSONL append with the boundary in step 2 of §The mandate above (atomic temp-rename + `os.replace` for full-file writes; `O_APPEND` POSIX line-atomicity ≤ PIPE_BUF guarantee for JSONL appends; `O_EXCL` for first-write no-clobber).

 137: def _mine_transcript(transcript_path):

 171:         p = _Path(transcript_path)
 172:         # F-003 path guard: require .jsonl suffix + transcript-like path
 173:         # (.claude/projects or subagents component)
 174:         path_str = str(p).replace("\\", "/").lower()
 175:         if not path_str.endswith(".jsonl"):
 176:             return ("", "", 0, "transcript_path_invalid")
 177:         if ".claude/projects" not in path_str and "subagents" not in path_str:
 178:             return ("", "", 0, "transcript_path_invalid")
 179:         if not p.exists():
 180:             return ("", "", 0, "transcript_missing")
 181:         # F-003 size guard: cap at 50 MB to avoid memory pressure on forged input
 182:         try:
 183:             size = p.stat().st_size
 184:         except (OSError, ValueError):
 185:             return ("", "", 0, "parse_failed")
 186:         if size > _TRANSCRIPT_MAX_BYTES:
 187:             return ("", "", 0, "transcript_too_large")
 188:         import json as _json
 189:         last_text = ""
 190:         stop_reason = ""
 191:         tool_uses = 0
 192:         nonblank_lines = 0
 193:         parsed_records = 0
 194:         json_error_count = 0
 195:         assistant_records = 0
 196:         # Stream line-by-line per F-001 + memory-pressure prescription
 197:         with p.open("r", encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as fh:
 198:             for raw in fh:
 199:                 stripped = raw.strip()
 200:                 if not stripped:
 201:                     continue
 202:                 nonblank_lines += 1
 203:                 try:
 204:                     rec = _json.loads(stripped)
 205:                 except _json.JSONDecodeError:
 206:                     json_error_count += 1
 207:                     continue
 208:                 if not isinstance(rec, dict):
 209:                     continue
 210:                 parsed_records += 1
 211:                 # F-002 assistant-message gate: only extract from assistant records
 212:                 rec_type = rec.get("type")
 213:                 msg = rec.get("message") if isinstance(rec.get("message"), dict) else None
 214:                 msg_role = msg.get("role") if msg else None
 215:                 is_assistant = (rec_type == "assistant") or (msg_role == "assistant")
 216:                 if not is_assistant:
 217:                     continue
 218:                 assistant_records += 1
 219:                 if msg:
 220:                     stop_r = msg.get("stop_reason")
 221:                     if isinstance(stop_r, str) and stop_r:
 222:                         stop_reason = stop_r
 223:                     content = msg.get("content")
 224:                     if isinstance(content, list):
 225:                         for blk in content:
 226:                             if isinstance(blk, dict):
 227:                                 btype = blk.get("type")
 228:                                 if btype == "tool_use":
 229:                                     tool_uses += 1
 230:                                 elif btype == "text":
 231:                                     txt = blk.get("text")
 232:                                     if isinstance(txt, str):
 233:                                         last_text = txt
 234:         # F-001 status discrimination: parse_empty (no usable assistant data)
 235:         # vs parse_failed (had nonblank lines but ALL parses failed)
 236:         if assistant_records == 0:
 237:             if nonblank_lines == 0 or parsed_records == 0:
 238:                 if nonblank_lines > 0 and parsed_records == 0:
 239:                     return ("", "", 0, "parse_failed")
 240:                 return ("", "", 0, "parse_empty")
 241:             return ("", "", 0, "parse_empty")
 242:         return (last_text[:2000], stop_reason, tool_uses, "ok")
 243:     except (OSError, ValueError):
 244:         return ("", "", 0, "parse_failed")

#!/usr/bin/env python3
# Reference: Z:/repos/deps/everything-claude-code/skills/continuous-learning-v2/SKILL.md:40-45,82-124 @ 841beea45cb25ba51f29fa45b7e272938d19b80a [content-SHA 5153a2aa688c37e5b515a3caff4adcdbe4de3964]
#   (instinct/observation pipeline and observations.jsonl hook capture) [VERIFIED 2026-04-28]
# Reference: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks (PostToolUse + Stop event observation contract)
# Reference (PARENT CONSUMER — TIER-3, not external SOTA): Z:/claude/scripts/observer_run.py (consumer schema — match)
# Reference (RUNTIME CACHE COPY — non-authority literal, not a cite): .claude/plugins/cache/everything-claude-code/everything-claude-code/1.10.0/skills/continuous-learning-v2/SKILL.md:82-124  # noqa: E501
# Reference (LOCAL SIBLING — TIER-3, not SOTA authority): Z:/claude-sota/.claude/hooks/scripts/_guard_base.py:_parse_stdin_json (TTY-safe stdin parser)
# Reference (LOCAL SIBLING — TIER-3, not SOTA authority): Z:/claude-sota/.claude/hooks/scripts/utils.py:log_swallow (sibling helper)
#
# Port-provenance (TIER-3, not SOTA authority): claude-sota Wave-B sequential-recovery from Z:/claude/hooks/_observation_writer.py @ 3267c25e
#   (parent snapshot 2026-04-27 from `git -C Z:/claude rev-parse HEAD`). Mechanical port — claude-sota's
#   _guard_base.py already exposes _parse_stdin_json (cycle-278 consolidation), so the import path
#   stays unchanged. OBSERVATIONS_JSONL adapts to claude-sota project root via Path(__file__) chain.
#   Per Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/port-note-discipline.md §2 Discipline 1: cite-line-bump n/a —
#   port-note is pure header insertion; symbol-anchor used for sibling cites (`:_parse_stdin_json`,
#   `:log_swallow`) per SOTA stability rationale.
"""Shared observation-writer for Karpathy L1->L2 producer hooks.

Cycle-147 H3-C1 fix for cycle-146 H2 Agent C finding #5: three H1 hooks
(tool_failure_observer.py, subagent_stop_telemetry.py, task_completed_observer.py)
shared ~150 LOC of identical scaffolding. This module consolidates:

- Path to observations.jsonl
- ISO timestamp helper
- stdin payload parse + fail-open contract
- observation append with mkdir
- stderr telemetry line

Contract preserved byte-for-byte: callers still emit valid JSONL rows with
the exact field shapes observer_run.py detectors expect. This is pure
refactor, no schema change.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import json
import os
import re
import sys
from datetime import UTC, datetime
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
from _guard_base import _parse_stdin_json  # noqa: E402
from utils import log_swallow  # noqa: E402

# claude-sota path adaptation: parent layout is Z:/claude/hooks/<file>.py with
# state at Z:/claude/.claude/state/. claude-sota layout is
# Z:/claude-sota/.claude/hooks/scripts/<file>.py with state at
# Z:/claude-sota/.claude/state/. Compute via two .parent hops vs parent's one.
OBSERVATIONS_JSONL = (
    Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent.parent / "state" / "observations.jsonl"
)

# Ship #221 — content-addressed stop_reason snapshots (codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION
# conf=0.90, applied per Pattern A; verdict at
# .claude/state/codex_consult_ship221_observations_stop_reason_snapshot_OUT.txt).
# F2 P1: hash raw UTF-8 stop_reason — NO timestamp canonicalization (unlike
# Ship #219 mcp_health) because stop_reason IS the event payload itself, not
# volatile probe noise. F4 P2: validated 64-hex sha gate prevents directory
# traversal in resolver. F3 P2: env-tunable threshold with strict fallback.
STOP_REASON_SNAPSHOT_DIR = OBSERVATIONS_JSONL.parent / "observation_stop_reasons"
_STOP_REASON_SCHEMA = "obs.v2.stop_reason_externalized"
_STOP_REASON_INLINE_THRESHOLD_DEFAULT = 2048
_STOP_REASON_PREVIEW_CHARS = 200
_STOP_REASON_SHA_RE = re.compile(r"^[0-9a-f]{64}$")
_STOP_REASON_THRESHOLD_ENV = "OBSERVATIONS_STOP_REASON_INLINE_THRESHOLD"


def now_iso() -> str:
    """Canonical UTC timestamp matching ECC continuous-learning-v2 schema."""
    return datetime.now(UTC).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")


def read_stdin_payload(disable_env: str, ctx: str) -> dict | None:
    """Parse stdin JSON payload with fail-open contract + DISABLE-env gate.

    Cycle-278: disable-env gate preserved at observer-layer; body delegates
    to `_guard_base._parse_stdin_json` for TTY-safe + broad-except semantics.

    Returns:
      dict if stdin was valid JSON dict
      None if: disable_env is set to "1", stdin empty, JSON malformed,
      or non-dict payload. Callers should `return 0` in any of those cases.
    """
    if os.environ.get(disable_env) == "1":
        return None
    return _parse_stdin_json(ctx)


def _stop_reason_inline_threshold() -> int:
    """Resolve threshold from env (codex T1 F3 P2 — validated env override).

    Invalid / negative values fall back to default 2048 chars (no silent
    misconfiguration that accidentally externalizes everything OR disables
    compaction entirely).
    """
    raw = os.environ.get(_STOP_REASON_THRESHOLD_ENV, "").strip()
    if not raw:
        return _STOP_REASON_INLINE_THRESHOLD_DEFAULT
    try:
        value = int(raw)
    except ValueError:
        return _STOP_REASON_INLINE_THRESHOLD_DEFAULT
    return value if value >= 0 else _STOP_REASON_INLINE_THRESHOLD_DEFAULT


def _externalize_stop_reason_if_needed(obs: dict, ctx: str) -> dict:
    """Externalize subagent_stop.stop_reason if > threshold; else passthrough.

    Per codex T1 F1 P1 (all-or-inline): snapshot MUST be written successfully
    BEFORE the compact pointer row is composed; on snapshot OSError, return
    the original obs UNMODIFIED so the inline stop_reason is preserved (no
    unrecoverable pointer in observations.jsonl).
    """
    if obs.get("type") != "subagent_stop":
        return obs
    stop_reason = obs.get("stop_reason")
    if not isinstance(stop_reason, str):
        return obs
    if len(stop_reason) <= _stop_reason_inline_threshold():
        return obs

    raw_bytes = stop_reason.encode("utf-8")
    sha = hashlib.sha256(raw_bytes).hexdigest()
    snap_path = STOP_REASON_SNAPSHOT_DIR / f"{sha}.txt"
    try:
        STOP_REASON_SNAPSHOT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        if not snap_path.is_file():
            with snap_path.open("x", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
                fh.write(stop_reason)
    except FileExistsError:
        # Race: another writer wrote the same content-addressed file. Idempotent.
        pass
    except OSError as exc:
        log_swallow(exc, f"{ctx}.stop_reason_snapshot")
        return obs

    compact = dict(obs)
    compact.pop("stop_reason", None)
    compact.update(
        {
            "schema": _STOP_REASON_SCHEMA,
            "stop_reason_sha": sha,
            "stop_reason_size": len(stop_reason),
            "stop_reason_preview": stop_reason[:_STOP_REASON_PREVIEW_CHARS],
        }
    )
    return compact


def resolve_stop_reason(record: dict, state_dir: Path | None = None) -> tuple[str | None, bool]:
    """Resolve stop_reason from inline OR content-addressed snapshot.

    Returns (text, snapshot_missing). For v1 inline → (text, False); for v2
    externalized with valid sha + readable snapshot → (text, False); for v2
    with invalid sha (not 64-hex) OR unreadable snapshot → (None, True).

    Per codex T1 F4 P2: validate stop_reason_sha as 64-hex digest BEFORE
    reading sidecar path — prevents directory-traversal via maliciously
    crafted JSONL records.
    """
    inline = record.get("stop_reason")
    if isinstance(inline, str):
        return inline, False

    sha = record.get("stop_reason_sha")
    if not isinstance(sha, str) or not _STOP_REASON_SHA_RE.fullmatch(sha):
        return None, record.get("schema") == _STOP_REASON_SCHEMA

    base_state_dir = state_dir if state_dir is not None else OBSERVATIONS_JSONL.parent
    snap_path = base_state_dir / "observation_stop_reasons" / f"{sha}.txt"
    try:
        return snap_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"), False
    except OSError:
        return None, True


def append_observation(obs: dict, ctx: str) -> bool:
    """Append one JSONL row. Returns True on success, False on I/O error.

    Fail-open: any OSError is log_swallow'd. Callers can still return 0.

    Ship #221: subagent_stop records with stop_reason > threshold are
    transparently externalized to .claude/state/observation_stop_reasons/<sha>.txt
    via _externalize_stop_reason_if_needed (raw UTF-8 SHA256 of the exact
    stop_reason text; no canonicalization per codex T1 F2). Other record
    types (subagent_start, task_completed) and short stop_reasons pass
    through unchanged.
    """
    try:
        OBSERVATIONS_JSONL.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        row = _externalize_stop_reason_if_needed(obs, ctx)
        with OBSERVATIONS_JSONL.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(json.dumps(row) + "\n")
        return True
    except OSError as exc:
        log_swallow(exc, f"{ctx}.observations_append")
        return False


def emit_telemetry(msg: str) -> None:
    """Write a one-line [hook-name] telemetry note to stderr."""
    sys.stderr.write(msg.rstrip() + "\n")
